People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Does it feel low or heavy then?\n\nPatient: When I first wake up… I’m not sure I’d call it low exactly. It’s more that I lie there for a bit trying to get my bearings. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear pattern. I’m sorry, is that the sort of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if most days feel emotionally heavy or low for you, even if it’s not constant throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s closer. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s this flatness. Even when my children sit with me, or the sun is out on my garden, things that should make me feel comforted or glad… I just don’t really feel it inside. It’s like that part of me has shut off. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention to that. I mean, I suppose I do sit longer before starting things, but I don’t know if that’s less energy or just… being unsettled. I can still do little things when I need to. Maybe slower than usual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can answer that properly. I do feel slower getting going, and little things can take me longer, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s my energy exactly. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just… hesitating more than usual. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel more tired than usual, even after resting or doing things that wouldn’t normally drain your energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I do rest, and sometimes I still don’t feel… ready, I suppose, but I’m not sure whether that’s tiredness or just me not getting myself together very well. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s a little more than usual, but I haven’t really noticed it clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m still not certain, doctor. I don’t want to give you the wrong idea. Some things do seem to take me a bit longer to start, but I can’t honestly say I’ve noticed a clear drop in energy. Maybe I’m just slower or pausing more. Is that a fair answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in activities you usually enjoy, like being with your children or spending time in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I don’t like saying it that way. My children can sit with me, and I know I ought to feel pleased or comforted, and when the sun is on the garden it still looks nice… but inside I feel sort of flat. Like the part of me that should feel glad has gone quiet. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood is low or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, doctor. I know I’ve been quieter and not quite myself, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds very definite, and I don’t want to say yes if that isn’t quite right. My mood is low at times, yes… but I’m not certain how much of the day it is. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just… hesitating more than usual. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel more tired than usual, even after resting or doing things that wouldn’t normally drain your energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. Maybe it’s a little more than usual, but I haven’t really noticed it clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m still not certain, doctor. Is that a fair answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in activities you usually enjoy, like being with your children or spending time in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I don’t like saying it that way. My children can sit with me, and I know I ought to feel pleased or comforted, and when the sun is on the garden it still looks nice… but inside I feel sort of flat. Like the part of me that should feel glad has gone quiet. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood is low or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, doctor. I know I’ve been quieter and not quite myself, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds very definite, and I don’t want to say yes if that isn’t quite right. My mood is low at times, yes… but I’m not certain how much of the day it is. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a definite loss of energy, where even small things feel more tiring than they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say there’s a definite loss, doctor. I mean, I do sit a bit longer before getting up to do things, but I haven’t really noticed small tasks feeling much more tiring than they used to. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that even getting started on small everyday tasks, like making tea or tidying up, feels more of a struggle than it used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, doctor. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I came because I just don’t feel quite like myself. Even when my children come and sit with me, or when the sun is nice on my garden, I feel sort of flat inside. Like the part of me that should feel glad, or comforted, has just… shut off. I’m not sure if that’s the right way to say it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About two weeks, I think. It started after my husband… collapsed, and the paramedics were working on him right there in our kitchen. I’m sorry, is that the sort of thing you mean? Since then, I haven’t really felt right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like being with your children or working in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s right. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even when my children sit with me, or when the light is nice out in the garden, I just feel sort of flat. Like I know I ought to feel glad or comforted, but that part of me doesn’t seem to come on. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I don’t want to overstate it. I’ve had sad moments, of course, but most of the day every day… I don’t know. It feels more like I’m dulled somehow, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you asked that already. I… I don’t think I can say yes to “most of the day, nearly every day.” I feel not myself, and there are moments I get very low, but “depressed” sounds like such a definite word. Maybe I’m just having trouble knowing what to call it. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what your mood is like in the morning when you first wake up? Does it feel low or heavy then?\n\nPatient: When I first wake up… I’m not sure I’d call it low exactly. It’s more that I lie there for a bit trying to get my bearings. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear pattern. I’m sorry, is that the sort of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if most days feel emotionally heavy or low for you, even if it’s not constant throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s closer. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s this flatness. Even when my children sit with me, or the sun is out on my garden, things that should make me feel comforted or glad… I just don’t really feel it inside. It’s like that part of me has shut off. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention to that. I mean, I suppose I do sit longer before starting things, but I don’t know if that’s less energy or just… being unsettled. I can still do little things when I need to. Maybe slower than usual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can answer that properly. I do feel slower getting going, and little things can take me longer, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s my energy exactly. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just… hesitating more than usual. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel more tired than usual, even after resting or doing things that wouldn’t normally drain your energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I do rest, and sometimes I still don’t feel… ready, I suppose, but I’m not sure whether that’s tiredness or just me not getting myself together very well. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s a little more than usual, but I haven’t really noticed it clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m still not certain, doctor. I don’t want to give you the wrong idea. Some things do seem to take me a bit longer to start, but I can’t honestly say I’ve noticed a clear drop in energy. Maybe I’m just slower or pausing more. Is that a fair answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in activities you usually enjoy, like being with your children or spending time in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I don’t like saying it that way. My children can sit with me, and I know I ought to feel pleased or comforted, and when the sun is on the garden it still looks nice… but inside I feel sort of flat. Like the part of me that should feel glad has gone quiet. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood is low or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, doctor. I know I’ve been quieter and not quite myself, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds very definite, and I don’t want to say yes if that isn’t quite right. My mood is low at times, yes… but I’m not certain how much of the day it is. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a definite loss of energy, where even small things feel more tiring than they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say there’s a definite loss, doctor. I mean, I do sit a bit longer before getting up to do things, but I haven’t really noticed small tasks feeling much more tiring than they used to. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that even getting started on small everyday tasks, like making tea or tidying up, feels more of a struggle than it used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, doctor. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I came because I just don’t feel quite like myself. Even when my children come and sit with me, or when the sun is nice on my garden, I feel sort of flat inside. Like the part of me that should feel glad, or comforted, has just… shut off. I’m not sure if that’s the right way to say it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About two weeks, I think. It started after my husband… collapsed, and the paramedics were working on him right there in our kitchen. I’m sorry, is that the sort of thing you mean? Since then, I haven’t really felt right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like being with your children or working in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s right. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even when my children sit with me, or when the light is nice out in the garden, I just feel sort of flat. Like I know I ought to feel glad or comforted, but that part of me doesn’t seem to come on. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I don’t want to overstate it. I’ve had sad moments, of course, but most of the day every day… I don’t know. It feels more like I’m dulled somehow, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you asked that already. I… I don’t think I can say yes to “most of the day, nearly every day.” I feel not myself, and there are moments I get very low, but “depressed” sounds like such a definite word. Maybe I’m just having trouble knowing what to call it. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what your mood is like in the morning when you first wake up? Does it feel low or heavy then?\n\nPatient: When I first wake up… I’m not sure I’d call it low exactly. It’s more that I lie there for a bit trying to get my bearings. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear pattern. I’m sorry, is that the sort of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if most days feel emotionally heavy or low for you, even if it’s not constant throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s closer. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s this flatness. Even when my children sit with me, or the sun is out on my garden, things that should make me feel comforted or glad… I just don’t really feel it inside. It’s like that part of me has shut off. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention to that. I mean, I suppose I do sit longer before starting things, but I don’t know if that’s less energy or just… being unsettled. I can still do little things when I need to. Maybe slower than usual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can answer that properly. I do feel slower getting going, and little things can take me longer, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s my energy exactly. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just… hesitating more than usual. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel more tired than usual, even after resting or doing things that wouldn’t normally drain your energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I do rest, and sometimes I still don’t feel… ready, I suppose, but I’m not sure whether that’s tiredness or just me not getting myself together very well. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s a little more than usual, but I haven’t really noticed it clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m still not certain, doctor. I don’t want to give you the wrong idea. Some things do seem to take me a bit longer to start, but I can’t honestly say I’ve noticed a clear drop in energy. Maybe I’m just slower or pausing more. Is that a fair answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in activities you usually enjoy, like being with your children or spending time in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I don’t like saying it that way. My children can sit with me, and I know I ought to feel pleased or comforted, and when the sun is on the garden it still looks nice… but inside I feel sort of flat. Like the part of me that should feel glad has gone quiet. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood is low or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, doctor. I know I’ve been quieter and not quite myself, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds very definite, and I don’t want to say yes if that isn’t quite right. My mood is low at times, yes… but I’m not certain how much of the day it is. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a definite loss of energy, where even small things feel more tiring than they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say there’s a definite loss, doctor. I mean, I do sit a bit longer before getting up to do things, but I haven’t really noticed small tasks feeling much more tiring than they used to. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that even getting started on small everyday tasks, like making tea or tidying up, feels more of a struggle than it used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, doctor. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still do make tea and tidy a little if it needs doing… maybe I’m slower to begin sometimes, but I haven’t really thought of it as a struggle exactly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that even starting small everyday tasks, like making tea or tidying up, feels more difficult or takes more effort than it used to?\n\nPatient: I suppose… no, I haven’t really noticed it in a definite way. I still get up and make tea, and if something needs tidying I can usually do it. Maybe I take a moment longer to get myself moving, but I don’t know that it feels more difficult exactly. I don’t want to overstate it. Is that all right?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you no longer enjoy things that used to bring you pleasure, like spending time with your children or being in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is true, doctor. I don’t like to say it, because my children are very kind, and the garden is lovely when the sun is on it. But even then, I feel sort of flat inside, as if the part of me that should feel glad or comforted just… isn’t working. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have completely lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like being with your children or spending time in your garden?\n\nPatient: Completely… I don’t know if I should say completely, doctor. I still love them, of course, and I like the idea of the garden. But that feeling I used to get… the gladness when my children sit with me, or comfort when the sun is on the garden… it just doesn’t seem to come. I feel flat inside, as if that part of me has shut off. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Even when my children come and sit with me, or when the sun is nice on my garden, I feel sort of flat inside. Like the part of me that should feel glad, or comforted, has just… shut off. I’m not sure if that’s the right way to say it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About two weeks, I think. It started after my husband… collapsed, and the paramedics were working on him right there in our kitchen. I’m sorry, is that the sort of thing you mean? Since then, I haven’t really felt right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like being with your children or working in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s right. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even when my children sit with me, or when the light is nice out in the garden, I just feel sort of flat. Like I know I ought to feel glad or comforted, but that part of me doesn’t seem to come on. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I don’t want to overstate it. I’ve had sad moments, of course, but most of the day every day… I don’t know. It feels more like I’m dulled somehow, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you asked that already. I… I don’t think I can say yes to “most of the day, nearly every day.” I feel not myself, and there are moments I get very low, but “depressed” sounds like such a definite word. Maybe I’m just having trouble knowing what to call it. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what your mood is like in the morning when you first wake up? Does it feel low or heavy then?\n\nPatient: When I first wake up… I’m not sure I’d call it low exactly. It’s more that I lie there for a bit trying to get my bearings. Some mornings feel heavier than others, I suppose, but I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear pattern. I’m sorry, is that the sort of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if most days feel emotionally heavy or low for you, even if it’s not constant throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s closer. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s this flatness. Even when my children sit with me, or the sun is out on my garden, things that should make me feel comforted or glad… I just don’t really feel it inside. It’s like that part of me has shut off. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention to that. I mean, I suppose I do sit longer before starting things, but I don’t know if that’s less energy or just… being unsettled. I can still do little things when I need to. Maybe slower than usual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can answer that properly. I do feel slower getting going, and little things can take me longer, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s my energy exactly. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just… hesitating more than usual. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel more tired than usual, even after resting or doing things that wouldn’t normally drain your energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I do rest, and sometimes I still don’t feel… ready, I suppose, but I’m not sure whether that’s tiredness or just me not getting myself together very well. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s a little more than usual, but I haven’t really noticed it clearly enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m still not certain, doctor. I don’t want to give you the wrong idea. Some things do seem to take me a bit longer to start, but I can’t honestly say I’ve noticed a clear drop in energy. Maybe I’m just slower or pausing more. Is that a fair answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in activities you usually enjoy, like being with your children or spending time in your garden?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I don’t like saying it that way. My children can sit with me, and I know I ought to feel pleased or comforted, and when the sun is on the garden it still looks nice… but inside I feel sort of flat. Like the part of me that should feel glad has gone quiet. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood is low or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, doctor. I know I’ve been quieter and not quite myself, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds very definite, and I don’t want to say yes if that isn’t quite right. My mood is low at times, yes… but I’m not certain how much of the day it is. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed a definite loss of energy, where even small things feel more tiring than they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say there’s a definite loss, doctor. I mean, I do sit a bit longer before getting up to do things, but I haven’t really noticed small tasks feeling much more tiring than they used to. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that even getting started on small everyday tasks, like making tea or tidying up, feels more of a struggle than it used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, doctor. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still do make tea and tidy a little if it needs doing… maybe I’m slower to begin sometimes, but I haven’t really thought of it as a struggle exactly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that even starting small everyday tasks, like making tea or tidying up, feels more difficult or takes more effort than it used to?\n\nPatient: I suppose… no, I haven’t really noticed it in a definite way. I don’t like to say it, because my children are very kind, and the garden is lovely when the sun is on it. But even then, I feel sort of flat inside, as if the part of me that should feel glad or comforted just… isn’t working. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have completely lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like being with your children or spending time in your garden?\n\nPatient: Completely… I don’t know if I should say completely, doctor. I still love them, of course, and I like the idea of the garden.
